   179→
   180→func (r *HookRunner) runHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   181→	// Run using the shared test binary
   182→	// Command structure: entire hooks claude-code <hook-name>
   183→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   184→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   185→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   186→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   187→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   188→	)
   189→
   190→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   191→	if err != nil {
   192→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   193→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   194→	}
   195→
   196→	r.T.Logf("Hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   197→	return nil
   198→}

   200→// Session represents a simulated Claude Code session.
   201→type Session struct {
   202→	ID                string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "test-session-1")
   203→	EntireID          string // Entire session ID with date prefix (e.g., "2025-12-02-test-session-1")
   204→	TranscriptPath    string
   205→	TranscriptBuilder *TranscriptBuilder
   206→	env               *TestEnv
   207→}
   208→
   209→// FileChange represents a file modification in a session.
   210→type FileChange struct {

   212→	Content string
   213→}
   214→
   215→// NewSession creates a new simulated session.
   216→func (env *TestEnv) NewSession() *Session {
   217→	env.T.Helper()
   218→
   219→	env.SessionCounter++
   220→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("test-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   221→	entireID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   222→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".jsonl")
   223→
   224→	return &Session{
   225→		ID:                sessionID,
   226→		EntireID:          entireID,
   227→		TranscriptPath:    transcriptPath,
   228→		TranscriptBuilder: NewTranscriptBuilder(),
   229→		env:               env,
   230→	}
   231→}
   232→
   233→// CreateTranscript builds and writes a transcript for the session.
   234→func (s *Session) CreateTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   235→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddUserMessage(prompt)
   236→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("I'll help you with that.")
   237→
   238→	for _, change := range changes {
   239→		toolID := s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolUse("mcp__acp__Write", change.Path, change.Content)
   240→		s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolResult(toolID)
   241→	}
   242→
   243→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("Done!")
   244→
   245→	if err := s.TranscriptBuilder.WriteToFile(s.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
   246→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	return s.TranscriptPath
   250→}

   327→// ClearSessionState removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   328→// This simulates what happens when a user commits their changes (session is "completed").
   329→// Used in tests to allow sequential sessions to run without triggering concurrent session warnings.
   330→func (env *TestEnv) ClearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
   331→	env.T.Helper()
   332→
   333→	// Session state is stored in .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
   334→	// Use sessionid.EntireSessionID to get the full session ID with date prefix
   335→	entireSessionID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   336→	stateFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions", entireSessionID+".json")
   337→
   338→	if err := os.Remove(stateFile); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {
   339→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to clear session state: %w", err)
   340→	}
   341→	return nil
   342→}

   344→// HookOutput contains the result of running a hook.
   345→type HookOutput struct {
   346→	Stdout []byte
   347→	Stderr []byte
   348→	Err    error

   350→
   351→// runHookWithOutput runs a hook and returns both stdout and stderr separately.
   352→func (r *HookRunner) runHookWithOutput(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) HookOutput {
   353→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   354→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   355→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   356→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   357→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   358→	)
   359→
   360→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   361→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   362→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   363→
   364→	err := cmd.Run()
   365→	return HookOutput{
   366→		Stdout: stdout.Bytes(),
   367→		Stderr: stderr.Bytes(),
   368→		Err:    err,
   369→	}
   370→}

   420→// GetSessionState reads and returns the session state for the given session ID.

   439→	return &state, nil

   453→// NewGeminiHookRunner creates a new Gemini hook runner for the given repo directory.
   454→func NewGeminiHookRunner(repoDir, geminiProjectDir string, t interface {
   455→	Helper()
   456→	Fatalf(format string, args ...interface{})
   457→	Logf(format string, args ...interface{})
   458→}) *GeminiHookRunner {
   459→	return &GeminiHookRunner{

   466→// runGeminiHookWithInput runs a Gemini hook with the given input.
   467→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookWithInput(hookName string, input interface{}) error {
   468→	r.T.Helper()
   469→
   470→	inputJSON, err := json.Marshal(input)
   471→	if err != nil {
   472→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hook input: %w", err)
   473→	}
   474→
   475→	return r.runGeminiHookInRepoDir(hookName, inputJSON)
   476→}
   477→
   478→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   479→	// Run using the shared test binary
   480→	// Command structure: entire hooks gemini <hook-name>
   481→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "gemini", hookName)
   482→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   483→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   484→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   485→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+r.GeminiProjectDir,
   486→	)
   487→
   488→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   489→	if err != nil {
   490→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   491→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   492→	}
   493→
   494→	r.T.Logf("Gemini hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   495→	return nil
   496→}

   590→// GeminiSession represents a simulated Gemini CLI session.
   591→type GeminiSession struct {
   592→	ID             string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "gemini-session-1")
   593→	EntireID       string // Entire session ID with date prefix (e.g., "2025-12-02-gemini-session-1")

   596→}
   597→
   598→// NewGeminiSession creates a new simulated Gemini session.
   599→func (env *TestEnv) NewGeminiSession() *GeminiSession {
   600→	env.T.Helper()
   601→
   602→	env.SessionCounter++
   603→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("gemini-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   604→	entireID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   605→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".json")
   606→
   607→	return &GeminiSession{
   608→		ID:             sessionID,
   609→		EntireID:       entireID,
   610→		TranscriptPath: transcriptPath,
   611→		env:            env,
   612→	}
   613→}
   614→
   615→// CreateGeminiTranscript creates a Gemini JSON transcript file for the session.
   616→func (s *GeminiSession) CreateGeminiTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   617→	// Build Gemini-format transcript (JSON, not JSONL)
   618→	messages := []map[string]interface{}{
   619→		{
   620→			"type":    "user",
   621→			"content": prompt,
   622→		},
   623→		{
   624→			"type":    "assistant",
   625→			"content": "I'll help you with that.",
   626→		},
   627→	}
   628→
   629→	for _, change := range changes {
   630→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   631→			"type": "tool_use",
   632→			"name": "write_file",
   633→			"input": map[string]string{
   634→				"path":    change.Path,
   635→				"content": change.Content,
   636→			},
   637→		})
   638→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   639→			"type":   "tool_result",
   640→			"output": "File written successfully",
   641→		})
   642→	}
   643→
   644→	messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   645→		"type":    "assistant",
   646→		"content": "Done!",
   647→	})
   648→
   649→	transcript := map[string]interface{}{
   650→		"sessionId": s.ID,
   651→		"messages":  messages,
   652→	}
   653→
   654→	// Ensure directory exists
   655→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(s.TranscriptPath), 0o755); err != nil {
   656→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to create transcript dir: %v", err)
   657→	}
   658→
   659→	// Write transcript
   660→	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(transcript, "", "  ")
   661→	if err != nil {
   662→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal transcript: %v", err)
   663→	}
   664→	if err := os.WriteFile(s.TranscriptPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   665→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   666→	}
   667→
   668→	return s.TranscriptPath
   669→}

    30→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    31→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.
    32→var testBinaryPath string
    33→
    34→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    35→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    36→func getTestBinary() string {
    37→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    38→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    39→	}
    40→	return testBinaryPath
    41→}
    42→
    43→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for integration tests.
    44→type TestEnv struct {
    45→	T                *testing.T
    46→	RepoDir          string
    47→	ClaudeProjectDir string
    48→	GeminiProjectDir string
    49→	SessionCounter   int
    50→}
    51→
    52→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated test environment.
    53→// It creates temp directories for the git repo and agent project files.
    54→// Note: Does NOT change working directory to allow parallel test execution.
    55→// Note: Does NOT use t.Setenv to allow parallel test execution - CLI commands
    56→// receive the env var via cmd.Env instead.
    57→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    58→	t.Helper()
    59→
    60→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    61→	// This ensures the CLI subprocess and test use consistent paths
    62→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    63→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    64→		repoDir = resolved
    65→	}
    66→	claudeProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    67→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(claudeProjectDir); err == nil {
    68→		claudeProjectDir = resolved
    69→	}
    70→	geminiProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    71→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(geminiProjectDir); err == nil {
    72→		geminiProjectDir = resolved
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	env := &TestEnv{
    76→		T:                t,
    77→		RepoDir:          repoDir,
    78→		ClaudeProjectDir: claudeProjectDir,
    79→		GeminiProjectDir: geminiProjectDir,
    80→	}
    81→
    82→	// Note: Don't use t.Setenv here - it's incompatible with t.Parallel()
    83→	// CLI commands receive ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR or ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR via cmd.Env instead
    84→
    85→	return env
    86→}
    87→
    88→// Cleanup is a no-op retained for backwards compatibility.
    89→//
    90→// Previously this method restored the working directory after NewTestEnv changed it.
    91→// With the refactor to remove os.Chdir from NewTestEnv:
    92→// - Temp directories are now cleaned up automatically by t.TempDir()
    93→// - Working directory is never changed, so no restoration is needed
    94→//
    95→// This method is kept to avoid breaking existing tests that call defer env.Cleanup().
    96→// New tests should not call this method as it serves no purpose.
    97→//
    98→// Deprecated: This method is a no-op and will be removed in a future version.
    99→func (env *TestEnv) Cleanup() {
   100→	// No-op - temp dirs are cleaned up by t.TempDir()
   101→}
   102→
   103→// cliEnv returns the environment variables for CLI execution.
   104→// Includes both Claude and Gemini project dirs so tests work for any agent.
   105→func (env *TestEnv) cliEnv() []string {
   106→	return append(os.Environ(),
   107→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+env.ClaudeProjectDir,
   108→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+env.GeminiProjectDir,
   109→	)
   110→}
   111→
   112→// RunCLI runs the entire CLI with the given arguments and returns stdout.
   113→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLI(args ...string) string {
   114→	env.T.Helper()
   115→	output, err := env.RunCLIWithError(args...)
   116→	if err != nil {
   117→		env.T.Fatalf("CLI command failed: %v\nArgs: %v\nOutput: %s", err, args, output)
   118→	}
   119→	return output
   120→}
   121→
   122→// RunCLIWithError runs the entire CLI and returns output and error.
   123→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLIWithError(args ...string) (string, error) {
   124→	env.T.Helper()
   125→
   126→	// Run CLI using the shared binary
   127→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), args...)
   128→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   129→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   130→
   131→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   132→	return string(output), err
   133→}
   134→
   135→// RunCLIWithStdin runs the CLI with stdin input.
   136→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLIWithStdin(stdin string, args ...string) string {
   137→	env.T.Helper()
   138→
   139→	// Run CLI with stdin using the shared binary
   140→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), args...)
   141→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   142→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   143→	cmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(stdin)
   144→
   145→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   146→	if err != nil {
   147→		env.T.Fatalf("CLI command failed: %v\nArgs: %v\nOutput: %s", err, args, output)
   148→	}
   149→	return string(output)
   150→}

   152→// NewRepoEnv creates a TestEnv with an initialized git repo and Entire.
   153→// This is a convenience factory for tests that need a basic repo setup.
   154→func NewRepoEnv(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   155→	t.Helper()
   156→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
   157→	env.InitRepo()
   158→	env.InitEntire(strategy)
   159→	return env
   160→}
   161→
   162→// NewRepoWithCommit creates a TestEnv with a git repo, Entire, and an initial commit.
   163→// The initial commit contains a README.md file.
   164→func NewRepoWithCommit(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   165→	t.Helper()
   166→	env := NewRepoEnv(t, strategy)
   167→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository")
   168→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
   169→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
   170→	return env
   171→}
   172→
   173→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates a TestEnv ready for session testing.
   174→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
   175→// and checks out a feature branch. This is the most common setup
   176→// for session and rewind tests since Entire tracking skips main/master.
   177→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
   178→	t.Helper()
   179→	env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strategyName)
   180→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/test-branch")
   181→	return env
   182→}
   183→
   184→// AllStrategies returns all strategy names for parameterized tests.
   185→func AllStrategies() []string {
   186→	return []string{
   187→		strategy.StrategyNameAutoCommit,
   188→		strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit,
   189→	}
   190→}
   191→
   192→// RunForAllStrategies runs a test function for each strategy in parallel.
   193→// This reduces boilerplate for tests that need to verify behavior across all strategies.
   194→// Each subtest gets its own TestEnv with a feature branch ready for testing.
   195→func RunForAllStrategies(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   196→	t.Helper()
   197→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   198→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   199→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   200→			t.Parallel()
   201→			env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strat)
   202→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   203→		})
   204→	}
   205→}
   206→
   207→// RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   208→// using NewRepoWithCommit instead of NewFeatureBranchEnv. Use this for tests
   209→// that need to test behavior on the main branch.
   210→func RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   211→	t.Helper()
   212→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   213→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   214→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   215→			t.Parallel()
   216→			env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strat)
   217→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   218→		})
   219→	}
   220→}
   221→
   222→// RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   223→// using NewRepoEnv (git repo + entire init, no commits). Use this for tests
   224→// that need to verify basic initialization behavior.
   225→func RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   226→	t.Helper()
   227→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   228→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   229→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   230→			t.Parallel()
   231→			env := NewRepoEnv(t, strat)
   232→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   233→		})
   234→	}
   235→}
   236→
   237→// RunForStrategiesSequential runs a test function for specific strategies sequentially.
   238→// Use this for tests that cannot be parallelized (e.g., tests using os.Chdir).
   239→// The strategies parameter allows testing a subset of strategies.
   240→func RunForStrategiesSequential(t *testing.T, strategies []string, testFn func(t *testing.T, strategyName string)) {
   241→	t.Helper()
   242→	for _, strat := range strategies {
   243→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   244→			testFn(t, strat)
   245→		})
   246→	}
   247→}
   248→
   249→// InitRepo initializes a git repository in the test environment.
   250→func (env *TestEnv) InitRepo() {
   251→	env.T.Helper()
   252→
   253→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(env.RepoDir, false)
   254→	if err != nil {
   255→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to init git repo: %v", err)
   256→	}
   257→
   258→	// Configure git user for commits
   259→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   260→	if err != nil {
   261→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   262→	}
   263→	cfg.User.Name = "Test User"
   264→	cfg.User.Email = "test@example.com"
   265→
   266→	// Disable GPG signing for test commits (prevents failures if user has commit.gpgsign=true globally)
   267→	if cfg.Raw == nil {
   268→		cfg.Raw = config.New()
   269→	}
   270→	cfg.Raw.Section("commit").SetOption("gpgsign", "false")
   271→
   272→	if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
   273→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
   274→	}
   275→}

   295→// InitEntireWithAgentAndOptions initializes Entire with the specified strategy, agent, and options.
   296→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithAgentAndOptions(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName, strategyOptions map[string]any) {

   300→
   301→// initEntireInternal is the common implementation for InitEntire variants.
   302→func (env *TestEnv) initEntireInternal(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   303→	env.T.Helper()
   304→
   305→	// Create .entire directory structure
   306→	entireDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire")
   307→	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   308→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire directory: %v", err)
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	// Create tmp directory
   312→	tmpDir := filepath.Join(entireDir, "tmp")
   313→	if err := os.MkdirAll(tmpDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   314→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire/tmp directory: %v", err)
   315→	}
   316→
   317→	// Write settings.json
   318→	settings := map[string]any{
   319→		"strategy":  strategyName,
   320→		"local_dev": true, // Use go run for hooks in tests
   321→	}
   322→	// Only add agent if specified (otherwise defaults to claude-code)
   323→	if agentName != "" {
   324→		settings["agent"] = string(agentName)
   325→	}
   326→	if strategyOptions != nil {
   327→		settings["strategy_options"] = strategyOptions
   328→	}
   329→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   330→	if err != nil {
   331→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal settings: %v", err)
   332→	}
   333→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(entireDir, paths.SettingsFileName)
   334→	if err := os.WriteFile(settingsPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   335→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write %s: %v", paths.SettingsFileName, err)
   336→	}
   337→}
   338→
   339→// WriteFile creates a file with the given content in the test repo.
   340→// It creates parent directories as needed.
   341→func (env *TestEnv) WriteFile(path, content string) {
   342→	env.T.Helper()
   343→
   344→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   345→
   346→	// Create parent directories
   347→	dir := filepath.Dir(fullPath)
   348→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
   349→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create directory %s: %v", dir, err)
   350→	}
   351→
   352→	if err := os.WriteFile(fullPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   353→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write file %s: %v", path, err)
   354→	}
   355→}
   356→
   357→// ReadFile reads a file from the test repo.
   358→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFile(path string) string {
   359→	env.T.Helper()
   360→
   361→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   362→	data, err := os.ReadFile(fullPath)
   363→	if err != nil {
   364→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read file %s: %v", path, err)
   365→	}
   366→	return string(data)
   367→}
   368→
   369→// ReadFileAbsolute reads a file using an absolute path.
   370→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileAbsolute(path string) string {
   371→	env.T.Helper()
   372→
   373→	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
   374→	if err != nil {
   375→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read file %s: %v", path, err)
   376→	}
   377→	return string(data)
   378→}

   380→// FileExists checks if a file exists in the test repo.
   381→func (env *TestEnv) FileExists(path string) bool {
   382→	env.T.Helper()
   383→
   384→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   385→	_, err := os.Stat(fullPath)
   386→	return err == nil
   387→}

   389→// GitAdd stages files for commit.
   390→func (env *TestEnv) GitAdd(paths ...string) {
   391→	env.T.Helper()
   392→
   393→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   394→	if err != nil {
   395→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   396→	}
   397→
   398→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   399→	if err != nil {
   400→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   401→	}
   402→
   403→	for _, path := range paths {
   404→		if _, err := worktree.Add(path); err != nil {
   405→			env.T.Fatalf("failed to add file %s: %v", path, err)
   406→		}
   407→	}
   408→}

   410→// GitCommit creates a commit with all staged files.
   411→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommit(message string) {
   412→	env.T.Helper()
   413→
   414→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   415→	if err != nil {
   416→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   417→	}
   418→
   419→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   420→	if err != nil {
   421→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   422→	}
   423→
   424→	_, err = worktree.Commit(message, &git.CommitOptions{
   425→		Author: &object.Signature{
   426→			Name:  "Test User",
   427→			Email: "test@example.com",
   428→			When:  time.Now(),
   429→		},
   430→	})
   431→	if err != nil {
   432→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   433→	}
   434→}

   496→// GitCommitWithMultipleSessions creates a commit with multiple Entire-Session trailers.
   497→// This simulates merge commits that combine work from multiple sessions.
   498→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithMultipleSessions(message string, sessionIDs []string) {
   499→	env.T.Helper()
   500→
   501→	// Format message with multiple session trailers
   502→	fullMessage := message + "\n\n"
   503→	var fullMessageSb404 strings.Builder
   504→	for _, sessionID := range sessionIDs {
   505→		fullMessageSb404.WriteString("Entire-Session: " + sessionID + "\n")
   506→	}
   507→	fullMessage += fullMessageSb404.String()
   508→
   509→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   510→	if err != nil {
   511→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   512→	}
   513→
   514→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   515→	if err != nil {
   516→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   517→	}
   518→
   519→	_, err = worktree.Commit(fullMessage, &git.CommitOptions{
   520→		Author: &object.Signature{
   521→			Name:  "Test User",
   522→			Email: "test@example.com",
   523→			When:  time.Now(),
   524→		},
   525→	})
   526→	if err != nil {
   527→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   528→	}
   529→}

   531→// GetHeadHash returns the current HEAD commit hash.
   532→func (env *TestEnv) GetHeadHash() string {
   533→	env.T.Helper()
   534→
   535→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   536→	if err != nil {
   537→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   538→	}
   539→
   540→	head, err := repo.Head()
   541→	if err != nil {
   542→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   543→	}
   544→
   545→	return head.Hash().String()
   546→}
   547→
   548→// GetShadowBranchName returns the worktree-specific shadow branch name for the current HEAD.
   549→// Format: entire/<commit[:7]>-<hash(worktreeID)[:6]>
   550→func (env *TestEnv) GetShadowBranchName() string {
   551→	env.T.Helper()
   552→
   553→	headHash := env.GetHeadHash()
   554→	worktreeID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(env.RepoDir)
   555→	if err != nil {
   556→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree ID: %v", err)
   557→	}
   558→	return checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(headHash, worktreeID)
   559→}
   560→
   561→// GetShadowBranchNameForCommit returns the worktree-specific shadow branch name for a given commit.
   562→// Format: entire/<commit[:7]>-<hash(worktreeID)[:6]>
   563→func (env *TestEnv) GetShadowBranchNameForCommit(commitHash string) string {
   564→	env.T.Helper()
   565→
   566→	worktreeID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(env.RepoDir)
   567→	if err != nil {
   568→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree ID: %v", err)
   569→	}
   570→	return checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(commitHash, worktreeID)
   571→}
   572→
   573→// GetGitLog returns a list of commit hashes from HEAD.
   574→func (env *TestEnv) GetGitLog() []string {
   575→	env.T.Helper()
   576→
   577→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   578→	if err != nil {
   579→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   580→	}
   581→
   582→	head, err := repo.Head()
   583→	if err != nil {
   584→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   585→	}
   586→
   587→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   588→	if err != nil {
   589→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get log: %v", err)
   590→	}
   591→
   592→	var commits []string
   593→	err = commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   594→		commits = append(commits, c.Hash.String())
   595→		return nil
   596→	})
   597→	if err != nil {
   598→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   599→	}
   600→
   601→	return commits
   602→}
   603→
   604→// GitCheckoutNewBranch creates and checks out a new branch.
   605→// Uses git CLI instead of go-git to work around go-git v5 bug where Checkout
   606→// deletes untracked files (see https://github.com/go-git/go-git/issues/970).
   607→func (env *TestEnv) GitCheckoutNewBranch(branchName string) {
   608→	env.T.Helper()
   609→
   610→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "checkout", "-b", branchName)
   611→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   612→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   613→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to checkout new branch %s: %v\nOutput: %s", branchName, err, output)
   614→	}
   615→}
   616→
   617→// GetCurrentBranch returns the current branch name.
   618→func (env *TestEnv) GetCurrentBranch() string {
   619→	env.T.Helper()
   620→
   621→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   622→	if err != nil {
   623→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   624→	}
   625→
   626→	head, err := repo.Head()
   627→	if err != nil {
   628→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   629→	}
   630→
   631→	if !head.Name().IsBranch() {
   632→		return "" // Detached HEAD
   633→	}
   634→
   635→	return head.Name().Short()
   636→}

   638→// RewindPoint mirrors strategy.RewindPoint for test assertions.
   639→type RewindPoint struct {
   640→	ID               string
   641→	Message          string
   642→	MetadataDir      string
   643→	Date             time.Time
   644→	IsTaskCheckpoint bool
   645→	ToolUseID        string
   646→	IsLogsOnly       bool
   647→	CondensationID   string
   648→}
   649→
   650→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   651→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   652→	env.T.Helper()
   653→
   654→	// Run rewind --list using the shared binary
   655→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   656→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   657→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   658→
   659→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   660→	if err != nil {
   661→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   662→	}
   663→
   664→	// Parse JSON output
   665→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   666→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   667→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   668→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   669→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   670→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   671→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   672→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   673→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   674→	}
   675→
   676→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   677→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   678→	}
   679→
   680→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   681→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   682→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   683→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   684→			ID:               jp.ID,
   685→			Message:          jp.Message,
   686→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   687→			Date:             date,
   688→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   689→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   690→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   691→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   692→		}
   693→	}
   694→
   695→	return points
   696→}

   754→// BranchExists checks if a branch exists in the repository.
   755→func (env *TestEnv) BranchExists(branchName string) bool {
   756→	env.T.Helper()
   757→
   758→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   759→	if err != nil {
   760→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   761→	}
   762→
   763→	refs, err := repo.References()
   764→	if err != nil {
   765→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
   766→	}
   767→
   768→	found := false
   769→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   770→		if ref.Name().Short() == branchName {
   771→			found = true
   772→		}
   773→		return nil
   774→	})
   775→
   776→	return found
   777→}

   797→// FileExistsInBranch checks if a file exists in a specific branch's tree.
   798→func (env *TestEnv) FileExistsInBranch(branchName, filePath string) bool {
   799→	env.T.Helper()
   800→
   801→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   802→	if err != nil {
   803→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   804→	}
   805→
   806→	// Get the branch reference
   807→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   808→	if err != nil {
   809→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   810→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   811→		if err != nil {
   812→			return false
   813→		}
   814→	}
   815→
   816→	// Get the commit
   817→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   818→	if err != nil {
   819→		return false
   820→	}
   821→
   822→	// Get the tree
   823→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   824→	if err != nil {
   825→		return false
   826→	}
   827→
   828→	// Check if file exists
   829→	_, err = tree.File(filePath)
   830→	return err == nil
   831→}
   832→
   833→// ReadFileFromBranch reads a file's content from a specific branch's tree.
   834→// Returns the content and true if found, empty string and false if not found.
   835→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileFromBranch(branchName, filePath string) (string, bool) {
   836→	env.T.Helper()
   837→
   838→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   839→	if err != nil {
   840→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   841→	}
   842→
   843→	// Get the branch reference
   844→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   845→	if err != nil {
   846→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   847→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   848→		if err != nil {
   849→			return "", false
   850→		}
   851→	}
   852→
   853→	// Get the commit
   854→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   855→	if err != nil {
   856→		return "", false
   857→	}
   858→
   859→	// Get the tree
   860→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   861→	if err != nil {
   862→		return "", false
   863→	}
   864→
   865→	// Get the file
   866→	file, err := tree.File(filePath)
   867→	if err != nil {
   868→		return "", false
   869→	}
   870→
   871→	// Get the content
   872→	content, err := file.Contents()
   873→	if err != nil {
   874→		return "", false
   875→	}
   876→
   877→	return content, true
   878→}

   904→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, simulating the prepare-commit-msg
   905→// and post-commit hooks as a human (with TTY). This is the default for tests.
   906→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   907→	env.T.Helper()
   908→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, true, files...)
   909→}
   910→
   911→// GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent is like GitCommitWithShadowHooks but simulates
   912→// an agent commit (no TTY). This triggers the fast path in PrepareCommitMsg that
   913→// skips content detection and interactive prompts for ACTIVE sessions.
   914→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent(message string, files ...string) {
   915→	env.T.Helper()
   916→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, false, files...)
   917→}
   918→
   919→// gitCommitWithShadowHooks is the shared implementation for committing with shadow hooks.
   920→// When simulateTTY is true, sets ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate a human at the terminal.
   921→// When false, filters it out to simulate an agent subprocess (no controlling terminal).
   922→func (env *TestEnv) gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, simulateTTY bool, files ...string) {
   923→	env.T.Helper()
   924→
   925→	// Stage files using go-git
   926→	for _, file := range files {
   927→		env.GitAdd(file)
   928→	}
   929→
   930→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
   931→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   932→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   933→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   934→	}
   935→
   936→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
   937→	// Pass source="message" to match real `git commit -m` behavior.
   938→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")
   939→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   940→	if simulateTTY {
   941→		// Simulate human at terminal: ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 makes hasTTY() return true
   942→		// and askConfirmTTY() return defaultYes without reading from /dev/tty.
   943→		prepCmd.Env = append(env.cliEnv(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
   944→	} else {
   945→		// Simulate agent: ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0 makes hasTTY() return false,
   946→		// triggering the fast path that adds trailers for ACTIVE sessions.
   947→		prepCmd.Env = append(env.cliEnv(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
   948→	}
   949→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   950→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   951→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   952→	}
   953→
   954→	// Read the modified message
   955→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   956→	if err != nil {
   957→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   958→	}
   959→
   960→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
   961→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   962→	if err != nil {
   963→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   964→	}
   965→
   966→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   967→	if err != nil {
   968→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   969→	}
   970→
   971→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
   972→		Author: &object.Signature{
   973→			Name:  "Test User",
   974→			Email: "test@example.com",
   975→			When:  time.Now(),
   976→		},
   977→	})
   978→	if err != nil {
   979→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   980→	}
   981→
   982→	// Run post-commit hook using the shared binary
   983→	// This triggers condensation if the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   984→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   985→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   986→	postCmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   987→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   988→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   989→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   990→	}
   991→}
   992→
   993→// GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks amends the last commit with shadow hooks.
   994→// This simulates `git commit --amend` with the prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks.
   995→// The prepare-commit-msg hook is called with "commit" source to indicate an amend.
   996→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   997→	env.T.Helper()
   998→
   999→	// Stage any additional files
  1000→	for _, file := range files {
  1001→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1002→	}
  1003→
  1004→	// Write commit message to temp file
  1005→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1006→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1007→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1008→	}
  1009→
  1010→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook with "commit" source (indicates amend).
  1011→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (amend is always a human operation).
  1012→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "commit")
  1013→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1014→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1015→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1016→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg (amend) output: %s", output)
  1017→	}
  1018→
  1019→	// Read the modified message
  1020→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1021→	if err != nil {
  1022→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1023→	}
  1024→
  1025→	// Amend the commit using go-git
  1026→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1027→	if err != nil {
  1028→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1029→	}
  1030→
  1031→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1032→	if err != nil {
  1033→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1034→	}
  1035→
  1036→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
  1037→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1038→			Name:  "Test User",
  1039→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1040→			When:  time.Now(),
  1041→		},
  1042→		Amend: true,
  1043→	})
  1044→	if err != nil {
  1045→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to amend commit: %v", err)
  1046→	}
  1047→
  1048→	// Run post-commit hook
  1049→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1050→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1051→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1052→		env.T.Logf("post-commit (amend) output: %s", output)
  1053→	}
  1054→}
  1055→
  1056→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
  1057→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
  1058→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
  1059→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
  1060→	env.T.Helper()
  1061→
  1062→	// Stage files using go-git
  1063→	for _, file := range files {
  1064→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1065→	}
  1066→
  1067→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
  1068→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1069→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1070→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1071→	}
  1072→
  1073→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
  1074→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (this tests the editor flow where
  1075→	// the user removes the trailer before committing).
  1076→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
  1077→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1078→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1079→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1080→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
  1081→	}
  1082→
  1083→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
  1084→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1085→	if err != nil {
  1086→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1087→	}
  1088→
  1089→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
  1090→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
  1091→	var cleanedLines []string
  1092→	for _, line := range lines {
  1093→		// Skip the trailer and the comments about it
  1094→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
  1095→			continue
  1096→		}
  1097→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
  1098→			continue
  1099→		}
  1100→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
  1101→			continue
  1102→		}
  1103→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
  1104→	}
  1105→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
  1106→
  1107→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
  1108→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1109→	if err != nil {
  1110→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1111→	}
  1112→
  1113→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1114→	if err != nil {
  1115→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1116→	}
  1117→
  1118→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
  1119→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1120→			Name:  "Test User",
  1121→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1122→			When:  time.Now(),
  1123→		},
  1124→	})
  1125→	if err != nil {
  1126→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
  1127→	}
  1128→
  1129→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
  1130→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1131→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1132→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1133→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1134→	}
  1135→}
  1136→
  1137→// ListBranchesWithPrefix returns all branches that start with the given prefix.
  1138→func (env *TestEnv) ListBranchesWithPrefix(prefix string) []string {
  1139→	env.T.Helper()
  1140→
  1141→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1142→	if err != nil {
  1143→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1144→	}
  1145→
  1146→	refs, err := repo.References()
  1147→	if err != nil {
  1148→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
  1149→	}
  1150→
  1151→	var branches []string
  1152→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
  1153→		name := ref.Name().Short()
  1154→		if len(name) >= len(prefix) && name[:len(prefix)] == prefix {
  1155→			branches = append(branches, name)
  1156→		}
  1157→		return nil
  1158→	})
  1159→
  1160→	return branches
  1161→}
  1162→
  1163→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
  1164→// This is used by tests that previously extracted the checkpoint ID from commit message trailers.
  1165→// Now that active branch commits are clean (no trailers), we get the ID from the sessions branch.
  1166→// Fatals if the checkpoint ID cannot be found, with detailed context about what was found.
  1167→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1168→	env.T.Helper()
  1169→
  1170→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1171→	if err != nil {
  1172→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1173→	}
  1174→
  1175→	// Get the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
  1176→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1177→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1178→	if err != nil {
  1179→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get %s branch: %v", paths.MetadataBranchName, err)
  1180→	}
  1181→
  1182→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1183→	if err != nil {
  1184→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit: %v", err)
  1185→	}
  1186→
  1187→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1188→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1189→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1190→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1191→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1192→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1193→		}
  1194→	}
  1195→
  1196→	env.T.Fatalf("could not find checkpoint ID in %s branch commit message:\n%s",
  1197→		paths.MetadataBranchName, commit.Message)
  1198→	return ""
  1199→}
  1200→
  1201→// TryGetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
  1202→// Returns empty string if the branch doesn't exist or has no checkpoint commits yet.
  1203→// Use this when you need to check if a checkpoint exists without failing the test.
  1204→func (env *TestEnv) TryGetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1205→	env.T.Helper()
  1206→
  1207→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1208→	if err != nil {
  1209→		return ""
  1210→	}
  1211→
  1212→	// Get the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
  1213→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1214→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1215→	if err != nil {
  1216→		return ""
  1217→	}
  1218→
  1219→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1220→	if err != nil {
  1221→		return ""
  1222→	}
  1223→
  1224→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1225→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1226→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1227→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1228→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1229→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1230→		}
  1231→	}
  1232→
  1233→	return ""
  1234→}
  1235→
  1236→// GetLatestCondensationID is an alias for GetLatestCheckpointID for backwards compatibility.
  1237→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCondensationID() string {
  1238→	return env.GetLatestCheckpointID()
  1239→}
  1240→
  1241→// GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage extracts the Entire-Checkpoint trailer from a commit message.
  1242→// Returns empty string if no trailer found.
  1243→func (env *TestEnv) GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitSHA string) string {
  1244→	env.T.Helper()
  1245→
  1246→	msg := env.GetCommitMessage(commitSHA)
  1247→	cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(msg)
  1248→	if !found {
  1249→		return ""
  1250→	}
  1251→	return cpID.String()
  1252→}
  1253→
  1254→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD on the active branch
  1255→// and returns the checkpoint ID from the first commit that has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
  1256→// This verifies that condensation actually happened (commit has trailer) without relying
  1257→// on timestamp-based matching.
  1258→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() string {
  1259→	env.T.Helper()
  1260→
  1261→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1262→	if err != nil {
  1263→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1264→	}
  1265→
  1266→	head, err := repo.Head()
  1267→	if err != nil {
  1268→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
  1269→	}
  1270→
  1271→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
  1272→	if err != nil {
  1273→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
  1274→	}
  1275→
  1276→	var checkpointID string
  1277→	//nolint:errcheck // ForEach callback handles errors
  1278→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
  1279→		if cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(c.Message); found {
  1280→			checkpointID = cpID.String()
  1281→			return errors.New("stop iteration") // Found it, stop
  1282→		}
  1283→		return nil
  1284→	})
  1285→
  1286→	if checkpointID == "" {
  1287→		env.T.Fatalf("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
  1288→	}
  1289→
  1290→	return checkpointID
  1291→}
  1292→
  1293→// ShardedCheckpointPath returns the sharded path for a checkpoint ID.
  1294→// Format: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>
  1295→// Delegates to id.CheckpointID.Path() for consistency.
  1296→func ShardedCheckpointPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1297→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path()
  1298→}
  1299→
  1300→// SessionFilePath returns the path to a session file within a checkpoint.
  1301→// Session files are stored in numbered subdirectories using 0-based indexing (e.g., 0/full.jsonl).
  1302→// This function constructs the path for the first (default) session.
  1303→func SessionFilePath(checkpointID string, fileName string) string {
  1304→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/0/" + fileName
  1305→}
  1306→
  1307→// CheckpointSummaryPath returns the path to the root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary) for a checkpoint.
  1308→func CheckpointSummaryPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1309→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/" + paths.MetadataFileName
  1310→}
  1311→
  1312→// SessionMetadataPath returns the path to the session-level metadata.json for a checkpoint.
  1313→func SessionMetadataPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1314→	return SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.MetadataFileName)
  1315→}
  1316→
  1317→func findModuleRoot() string {
  1318→	// Start from this source file's location and walk up to find go.mod
  1319→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
  1320→	if !ok {
  1321→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
  1322→	}
  1323→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
  1324→
  1325→	for {
  1326→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
  1327→			return dir
  1328→		}
  1329→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
  1330→		if parent == dir {
  1331→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
  1332→		}
  1333→		dir = parent
  1334→	}
  1335→}
  1336→
```

```
     1→# Remove transcript_path Storage and Compute Dynamically
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→Currently, we store `transcript_path` in session state for each agent session. The transcript path is provided by the agent (Claude or Gemini) in every hook call and stored in:
     6→- `session.State.TranscriptPath` (in `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`)
     7→- Used for mid-session commit detection to analyze live transcript files
     8→
     9→**The Problem:**
    10→Storing the transcript path is unnecessary because both Claude and Gemini use deterministic path schemes that can be computed from:
    11→1. **Home directory** (`os.UserHomeDir()`)
    12→2. **Repository path** (repo root)
    13→3. **Session ID** (agent session UUID)
    14→4. **Agent type** (to select the right path formula)
    15→
    16→For Gemini, we'd also need the session start time, but that's already stored in `State.StartedAt`.
    17→
    18→**Why this change:**
    19→- Eliminates redundant storage
    20→- Makes session data more portable (not tied to specific file system paths)
    21→- Follows the pattern already used by `Agent.GetSessionDir(repoPath)` which computes paths deterministically
    22→- Simplifies session initialization by removing one field to track
    23→
    24→## Current State
    25→
    26→### How transcript_path flows currently:
    27→
    28→1. **Hook provides transcript_path** (from agent's JSON payload):
    29→   - Claude: `transcript_path` in `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, etc. hooks
    30→   - Gemini: `transcript_path` in `BeforeAgent`, `AfterAgent`, etc. hooks
    31→
    32→2. **Stored in session state** during `InitializeSession()`:
    33→   - `/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go:1238-1239` - updates on session resume
    34→   - `/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go:232` - sets on new session
    35→   - `/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go:949` - sets in auto-commit strategy
    36→
    37→3. **Used for mid-session commit detection**:
    38→   - `/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go:967-1005` (`sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript`)
    39→   - Calls `agent.TranscriptAnalyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)`
    40→   - Calls `agent.TranscriptAnalyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, ...)`
    41→
    42→4. **Used during condensation**:
    43→   - `/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_condensation.go:126` - passed to `extractSessionData`
    44→   - Condensation prefers live transcript over shadow branch copy for freshness
    45→
    46→### Agent-specific path schemes:
    47→
    48→**Claude Code** (`/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/claude.go:165-179`):
    49→```
    50→$HOME/.claude/projects/<sanitized-repo-path>/<session-id>.jsonl
    51→```
    52→- `sanitized-repo-path` = repo path with non-alphanumeric chars replaced by `-`
    53→- Session ID is the agent's UUID (identity with Entire session ID)
    54→- Extension: `.jsonl`
    55→
    56→**Gemini CLI** (`/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/gemini.go:173-189`):
    57→```
    58→$HOME/.gemini/tmp/<sanitized-repo-path>/chats/session-<date>-<id-prefix>.json
    59→```
    60→- `sanitized-repo-path` = repo path with non-alphanumeric chars replaced by `-` (same as Claude)
    61→- Date format: `YYYY-MM-DDTHH-MM` (from session start time)
    62→- ID prefix: first portion of session UUID
    63→- Extension: `.json`
    64→
    65→Note: Based on the comment in `/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/transcript.go:12`, Gemini uses:
    66→```
    67→~/.gemini/tmp/<hash>/chats/session-<date>-<id>.json
    68→```
    69→
    70→The `<hash>` in the comment suggests a hash-based directory name, but the actual implementation in `SanitizePathForGemini()` uses the same dash-replacement as Claude, not a SHA256 hash like mentioned in the user's request.
    71→
    72→## Proposed Changes
    73→
    74→### 1. Add transcript path computation methods to Agent interface
    75→
    76→**File:** `/cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go`
    77→
    78→Add new method to `Agent` interface:
    79→```go
    80→// ComputeTranscriptPath returns the path where this agent stores session transcripts.
    81→// Parameters:
    82→//   - repoPath: absolute path to repository root
    83→//   - sessionID: the agent's session UUID (NOT the legacy date-prefixed Entire ID)
    84→//   - startTime: session start time (used for Gemini's date-based naming)
    85→// Returns the absolute path to the transcript file.
    86→ComputeTranscriptPath(repoPath, sessionID string, startTime time.Time) (string, error)
    87→```
    88→
    89→### 2. Implement ComputeTranscriptPath for each agent
    90→
    91→**Claude Code** (`/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/claude.go`):
    92→```go
    93→// ComputeTranscriptPath returns the path to a Claude Code session transcript.
    94→// Format: $HOME/.claude/projects/<sanitized-repo-path>/<session-id>.jsonl
    95→func (c *ClaudeCodeAgent) ComputeTranscriptPath(repoPath, sessionID string, startTime time.Time) (string, error) {
    96→	sessionDir, err := c.GetSessionDir(repoPath)
    97→	if err != nil {
    98→		return "", err
    99→	}
   100→	return filepath.Join(sessionDir, sessionID+".jsonl"), nil
   101→}
   102→```
   103→
   104→**Gemini CLI** (`/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/gemini.go`):
   105→
   106→User clarified:
   107→- **Path format:** Use SHA256 hash of repo path (not dash replacement)
   108→- **Session ID in filename:** First block until `-` is reached (first segment of UUID)
   109→
   110→Example UUID: `a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890`
   111→→ First block: `a1b2c3d4`
   112→
   113→```go
   114→// ComputeTranscriptPath returns the path to a Gemini CLI session transcript.
   115→// Format: $HOME/.gemini/tmp/<sha256-hash>/chats/session-<date>-<id-prefix>.json
   116→// Date format: YYYY-MM-DDTHH-MM (from startTime)
   117→// ID prefix: First block of UUID (up to first dash)
   118→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) ComputeTranscriptPath(repoPath, sessionID string, startTime time.Time) (string, error) {
   119→	homeDir, err := os.UserHomeDir()
   120→	if err != nil {
   121→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get home directory: %w", err)
   122→	}
   123→
   124→	// SHA256 hash of repo path
   125→	hash := sha256.Sum256([]byte(repoPath))
   126→	projectHash := hex.EncodeToString(hash[:])
   127→
   128→	// Extract first block of session ID (up to first dash)
   129→	idPrefix := sessionID
   130→	if dashIdx := strings.IndexByte(sessionID, '-'); dashIdx > 0 {
   131→		idPrefix = sessionID[:dashIdx]
   132→	}
   133→
   134→	// Format: session-2026-02-11T00-20-a1b2c3d4.json
   135→	dateStr := startTime.Format("2006-01-02T15-04")
   136→	filename := fmt.Sprintf("session-%s-%s.json", dateStr, idPrefix)
   137→
   138→	return filepath.Join(homeDir, ".gemini", "tmp", projectHash, "chats", filename), nil
   139→}
   140→```
   141→
   142→**Important:** This changes the current `GetSessionDir()` implementation which uses `SanitizePathForGemini()`. We need to update `GetSessionDir()` to also use SHA256 hashing to be consistent.
   143→

   148→Remove:

   151→TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path,omitempty"`
   152→```
   153→
   154→Keep the JSON tag `transcript_path` deprecated in comments for backward compatibility documentation.

   243→// GetSessionDir returns the directory where Gemini stores session transcripts.
   244→// Gemini stores sessions in ~/.gemini/tmp/<sha256-hash>/chats/
   245→func (g *GeminiCLIAgent) GetSessionDir(repoPath string) (string, error) {

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me compile my findings:

## Comprehensive Report: Session Log Restoration in the Codebase

Based on my thorough code search, here's where and how session logs are restored in this codebase:

### **1. Key Log Restoration Entry Points**

#### **Resume Command** (`cmd/entire/cli/resume.go`)
- **Main function**: `resumeSession()` (line 358)
- **Flow**: 
  1. Detects agent via `agent.Detect()` 
  2. Gets session directory via `ag.GetSessionDir(repoRoot)` 
  3. Calls `strategy.RestoreLogsOnly(point, force)` (line 405)
  4. For multi-session checkpoints, reads all session transcripts and displays resume commands

#### **RestoreLogsOnly Implementation** (`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_rewind.go` lines 619-740)
- **Core logic**:
  - Reads `CheckpointSummary` via `store.ReadCommitted()`
  - Determines session count and validates timestamps
  - For each session (0-based indexing), calls `store.ReadSessionContent()`
  - Writes restored transcript to agent's session directory using `os.WriteFile()`
  - **Key function**: `classifySessionsForRestore()` (line 792) - checks if local logs are newer than checkpoint before restoring

### **2. Where `ComputeTranscriptPath` is Used**

Currently, `ComputeTranscriptPath` is **referenced but NOT directly used for log restoration**. Instead:

- **Hook handlers pass transcript paths directly** via `input.SessionRef` (agent's `ParseHookInput()`)
  - `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` line 82: `hookData.input.SessionRef`
  - `hooks_geminicli_handlers.go` line 440: `input.SessionRef`
  
- **Session state stores the transcript path** in `state.TranscriptPath`
  - `manual_commit_hooks.go` line 1005: Used to read live transcript for mid-session commit detection
  - `manual_commit_hooks.go` line 1238-1239: Updated from hook input

- **For restoration from checkpoints**: The transcript is read FROM GIT (not from agent's storage)
  - `checkpoint.committed.go` lines 721-724: `readTranscriptFromTree()` reads from git tree
  - `manual_commit_rewind.go` line 694: `store.ReadSessionContent()` fetches from git

### **3. Two Different Transcript Reading Flows**

**Flow A: Restoring Checkpoints (Using Git)**
```
RestoreLogsOnly()
  → store.ReadSessionContent(checkpointID, sessionIndex)
    → tree.File(paths.TranscriptFileName) [from entire/checkpoints/v1 branch]
    → os.WriteFile(claudeSessionFile, transcript, 0o600)
```

**Flow B: Mid-Session Transcript Analysis (Using Agent's Storage)**
```
sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()
  → analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)
  → analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, offset)
```

### **4. Key Files for Log Restoration**

| File | Purpose | Key Functions |
|------|---------|---|
| `resume.go` | Resume command entry point | `resumeSession()`, `resumeSingleSession()` |
| `manual_commit_rewind.go` | Rewind/restore implementation | `RestoreLogsOnly()`, `classifySessionsForRestore()` |
| `committed.go` | Git-based checkpoint storage | `ReadSessionContent()`, `LookupSessionLog()` |
| `claudecode/claude.go` | Claude agent implementation | `ComputeTranscriptPath()` (line 181-189), `ReadSession()`, `WriteSession()` |
| `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` | Hook handlers | `captureInitialState()` (line 74), `commitWithMetadata()` |

### **5. Current Transcript Path Usage Pattern**

**During Sessions** (Mid-session work):
```go
// From hook input (agent provides the path)
transcriptPath := input.SessionRef  // e.g., ~/.claude/projects/<repo>/UUID.jsonl

// Stored in session state
state.TranscriptPath = transcriptPath

// Used for live content detection
analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)
analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, offset)
```

**During Resume** (After session ends):
```go
// Read from git (not from agent's storage)
content := store.ReadSessionContent(checkpointID, sessionIndex)

// Write to agent's storage
os.WriteFile(claudeProjectDir/sessionID.jsonl, content.Transcript, 0o600)
```

### **6. Where `ComputeTranscriptPath` Should Be Used**

Based on the code analysis, `ComputeTranscriptPath` is **NOT currently used in log restoration** because:

1. **Hook handlers already receive the transcript path** from the agent via `input.SessionRef`
2. **Session state persists the transcript path** from hooks
3. **Restoration reads from git**, not from agent storage

However, `ComputeTranscriptPath` could be relevant for:
- **Future session resume from disk** (if we wanted to restore directly from agent's storage without git)
- **Session recovery** (computing where transcript *should* be if missing)
- **Validation** (verifying agent session path format)

### **7. Key Observation: No Direct Restoration from Agent Storage**

The codebase **does NOT restore session transcripts directly from the agent's file storage** (e.g., `~/.claude/projects/*/UUID.jsonl`). Instead:

1. When a session is saved, the **entire transcript is copied to git** (via `commitWithMetadata()`)
2. On resume, it's **restored FROM GIT back to the agent's storage** (via `RestoreLogsOnly()`)
3. This ensures checkpoints are the source of truth, not agent storage

This is actually **more robust** than using `ComputeTranscriptPath` to restore directly, since it prevents inconsistencies between git checkpoints and agent storage.

   619→// RestoreLogsOnly restores session logs from a logs-only rewind point.
   620→// This fetches the transcript from entire/checkpoints/v1 and writes it to Claude's project directory.
   621→// Does not modify the working directory.
   622→// When multiple sessions were condensed to the same checkpoint, ALL sessions are restored.
   623→// If force is false, prompts for confirmation when local logs have newer timestamps.
   624→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) RestoreLogsOnly(point RewindPoint, force bool) error {
   625→	if !point.IsLogsOnly {
   626→		return errors.New("not a logs-only rewind point")
   627→	}
   628→
   629→	if point.CheckpointID.IsEmpty() {
   630→		return errors.New("missing checkpoint ID")
   631→	}
   632→
   633→	// Get checkpoint store
   634→	store, err := s.getCheckpointStore()
   635→	if err != nil {
   636→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get checkpoint store: %w", err)
   637→	}
   638→
   639→	// Read checkpoint summary to get session count
   640→	summary, err := store.ReadCommitted(context.Background(), point.CheckpointID)
   641→	if err != nil {
   642→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to read checkpoint: %w", err)
   643→	}
   644→	if summary == nil {
   645→		return fmt.Errorf("checkpoint not found: %s", point.CheckpointID)
   646→	}
   647→
   648→	// Get repo root for Claude project path lookup
   649→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   650→	if err != nil {
   651→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get repository root: %w", err)
   652→	}
   653→
   654→	claudeProjectDir, err := paths.GetClaudeProjectDir(repoRoot)
   655→	if err != nil {
   656→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get Claude project directory: %w", err)
   657→	}
   658→
   659→	// Ensure project directory exists
   660→	if err := os.MkdirAll(claudeProjectDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   661→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create Claude project directory: %w", err)
   662→	}
   663→
   664→	// Check for newer local logs if not forcing
   665→	if !force {
   666→		sessions := s.classifySessionsForRestore(context.Background(), claudeProjectDir, store, point.CheckpointID, summary)
   667→		hasConflicts := false
   668→		for _, sess := range sessions {
   669→			if sess.Status == StatusLocalNewer {
   670→				hasConflicts = true
   671→				break
   672→			}
   673→		}
   674→		if hasConflicts {
   675→			shouldOverwrite, promptErr := PromptOverwriteNewerLogs(sessions)
   676→			if promptErr != nil {
   677→				return promptErr
   678→			}
   679→			if !shouldOverwrite {
   680→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Resume cancelled. Local session logs preserved.\n")
   681→				return nil
   682→			}
   683→		}
   684→	}
   685→
   686→	// Count sessions to restore
   687→	totalSessions := len(summary.Sessions)
   688→	if totalSessions > 1 {
   689→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Restoring %d sessions from checkpoint:\n", totalSessions)
   690→	}
   691→
   692→	// Restore all sessions (oldest to newest, using 0-based indexing)
   693→	for i := range totalSessions {
   694→		content, readErr := store.ReadSessionContent(context.Background(), point.CheckpointID, i)
   695→		if readErr != nil {
   696→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Warning: failed to read session %d: %v\n", i, readErr)
   697→			continue
   698→		}
   699→		if content == nil || len(content.Transcript) == 0 {
   700→			continue
   701→		}
   702→
   703→		sessionID := content.Metadata.SessionID
   704→		if sessionID == "" {
   705→			// Fallback: can't identify session without ID
   706→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Warning: session %d has no session ID, skipping\n", i)
   707→			continue
   708→		}
   709→
   710→		modelSessionID := sessionid.ModelSessionID(sessionID)
   711→		claudeSessionFile := filepath.Join(claudeProjectDir, modelSessionID+".jsonl")
   712→
   713→		// Get first prompt for display
   714→		promptPreview := ExtractFirstPrompt(content.Prompts)
   715→
   716→		if totalSessions > 1 {
   717→			isLatest := i == totalSessions-1
   718→			if promptPreview != "" {
   719→				if isLatest {
   720→					fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Session %d (latest): %s\n", i+1, promptPreview)
   721→				} else {
   722→					fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Session %d: %s\n", i+1, promptPreview)
   723→				}
   724→			}
   725→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "    Writing to: %s\n", claudeSessionFile)
   726→		} else {
   727→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Writing transcript to: %s\n", claudeSessionFile)
   728→		}
   729→
   730→		if writeErr := os.WriteFile(claudeSessionFile, content.Transcript, 0o600); writeErr != nil {
   731→			if totalSessions > 1 {
   732→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "    Warning: failed to write transcript: %v\n", writeErr)
   733→				continue
   734→			}
   735→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to write transcript: %w", writeErr)
   736→		}
   737→	}
   738→
   739→	return nil
   740→}
   741→
   742→// readSessionPrompt reads the first prompt from the session's prompt.txt file stored in git.
   743→// Returns an empty string if the prompt cannot be read.
   744→func readSessionPrompt(repo *git.Repository, commitHash plumbing.Hash, metadataDir string) string {
   745→	// Get the commit and its tree
   746→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(commitHash)
   747→	if err != nil {
   748→		return ""
   749→	}
   750→
   751→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   752→	if err != nil {
   753→		return ""
   754→	}
   755→
   756→	// Look for prompt.txt in the metadata directory
   757→	promptPath := metadataDir + "/" + paths.PromptFileName
   758→	promptEntry, err := tree.File(promptPath)
   759→	if err != nil {
   760→		return ""
   761→	}
   762→
   763→	content, err := promptEntry.Contents()
   764→	if err != nil {
   765→		return ""
   766→	}
   767→
   768→	return ExtractFirstPrompt(content)
   769→}

   771→// SessionRestoreStatus represents the status of a session being restored.
   772→type SessionRestoreStatus int
   773→

   776→	StatusUnchanged                                   // Local and checkpoint are the same
   777→	StatusCheckpointNewer                             // Checkpoint has newer entries
   778→	StatusLocalNewer                                  // Local has newer entries (conflict)
   779→)
